115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳 、暫繳稅款 俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件 、資通與視聽產品 等進口代徵稅額增加 ，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

### Page 3

3
4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口 車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外 ，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額534 億 元， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

### Page 4

4
三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 9 月中央政府賦稅收入 (不含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵淨 額
2 兆 3,603 億元(占分配預算數 119.3%)，較上年同期增加 2,553 億元
(+12.1%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,748 億元(-90.7%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 3,077
億元、2,230 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 9 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵
淨額 1 兆 898 億元(占分配預
算數 109.4%)，較上年同期增
加 4,527 億元(+71.0%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配 之營業稅
增加 4,030 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 9 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 195 億元，較
上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.8%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 135
億元，較上年同期減少 7 億元(-4.7%)；撥入長照基金 883 億元(其中 541

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、195 億元來自菸稅，146 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 8 億元(+0.9%)。

2026. 10.20
9月小売売上高は実質減も10月以降の年末商戦
は堅調予想。鉱工業生産は増産基調を維持。物
価は原油高や関税の影響から消費者物価が加速、
生産者物価も大幅上昇し、インフレへの警戒強まる

経済が伸び悩む一方で、インフレ圧力の強まりを確
認する結果。8月鉱工業生産は2カ月連続で減少。

転化。対して9月消費者物価は前月から伸び加速
欧
州
7～9月期の実質GDPは前年比＋4.5％と小幅加
速。中国政府はインフラ投資を加速させ、通年の成
⾧率目標「＋4.5～5.0％」の確保を狙う。9月の輸
出はAIブームと自動車の輸出攻勢で伸び加速
中
国
8月機械受注は市場予想を上回り、設備投資の先
行きに明るさ。鉱工業生産は確報値で下方修正さ

れ、減産幅が拡大。9月国内企業物価は前月から
減速したものの、依然として高い伸びが継続
日
本
先週（10/12～10/19）発表の主な経済指標
世界的なAI投資ブームが日本にも波及、設備投資需要が拡大。
ブームを受けて銅やレアメタルなどの鉱物需要も増加。資源開発
投資の回復は鈍く、将来の需給逼迫やインフレ圧力要因に
IMFは市場の底堅さを評価も、金融安定リスクへの警戒を維持。
ヘッジファンドの分析では、レバレッジや取引集中による相場変動
の増幅、プライムブローカーを通じた銀行部門への波及を警戒
注目点② 金融市場に潜むリスクの増幅経路注目点①AI投資拡大と資源供給制約

日本：7月以降の設備投資需要はAI関連産業を中心に旺盛
3
 7～9月期の機械受注は増勢回復へ。AI関連業種からの受注がけん引
― ①旺盛なAI需要のもと、関連業種では設備不足感が強まっていたこと、②輸出増が持続するもとで、好業績が期待され
ること、③中東情勢の不確実性が後退したことなどが背景
 先行き設備投資は緩やかな増勢を辿る見込み
― 半導体製造装置をはじめ、AI・データセンター関連品目を中心に受注残が増加しており、目先こうした傾向が続く見通し
― 積み上がった受注残を段階的に消化することで、設備投資が持続的に下支えされる公算が大きい

（注） 季節調整値。最終需要・中間需要物価指数（国内品資本財）を用いて実質化。
左図の26年7～9月期（破線部）は内閣府見通しを基にした推計値。
右図の26年7～9月期は、同7～8月平均の数値
（出所） 内閣府「機械受注統計」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

需要物価指数（国内品資本財）で実質化したもの

世界：AI関連投資の拡大が鉱物需要を押し上げる一方、鈍い資源開発投資の回復ペース
4
 AI関連需要の拡大が銅やレアアースの需給環境に影響
― データセンター建設や電力インフラ整備に伴い、銅やレアアースの需要が拡大。ガリウム等の戦略鉱物への需要も増加
 資源開発投資の回復が遅れるなか、中長期的な供給不足への懸念強まる
―IEAは、2035年に銅が供給量対比で約26％の供給不足に陥ると予測。レアアースは供給不足を回避する見込みながら、

採掘・精錬において中国が支配的地位を占めており、供給元の集中がリスク
IEA：銅、レアアースの需要見通し IEA：銅・レアアースの需給バランス予測（2035年）
（出所）IEA "Global Critical Minerals Outlook 2026"より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

世界：ヘッジファンドのレバレッジ拡大が市場ショックを増幅、銀行部門への波及にも警戒

― ヘッジファンドの総資産は2013年の約4兆ドルから2026年初には約13兆ドルに拡大。平時には市場の流動性や効率性
を支える一方、ストレス時にはレバレッジ解消や取引集中が価格変動を増幅させる懸念

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

― 石油燃料の輸出量は同＋39.3％の716万トンで、2022年12月以来の高水準。引き合いの増加に応えた形
 輸入は同＋31.0％（前月：＋28.2％）。AIブームの価格要因で高水準を維持も、資源・エネルギーの輸入量は伸びず

― コンピューター（同＋296.5％）、集積回路（同＋74.7％）が好調。輸入単価の伸び（集積回路：同＋71.8％）が顕著
― 原油の輸入量は同▲21.5％の3,710万トン。中東危機発生後、2ケタ減が継続しており、代替調達の動き見えず

 ASEAN5では石油の国家備蓄制度や設備が未整備。石油の安定供給に向けた対応の強化が急務
―ASEANの場合、石油備蓄量は1～2カ月分と限定的。緊急時を見据えた石油備蓄の整備は途上
―10月のAZEC閣僚会合においては、ASEANの石油備蓄拡大や、緊急時の共同備蓄放出に向けた計画などで合意
 電力については域内での需要拡大が見込まれる中、発電能力の増強や送配電網の整備による電力供給の拡大が課題
―IEAは東南アジアの電力需要が2030年まで年平均5.4％増加すると予測。産業や冷房、データセンター需要が押し上げ
― 再エネ含む多様な電源による供給拡大や、AEBFでも議論された国際送配電網の整備や電力融通の取り組みが主眼に

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ

 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み

市場焦點
美國
• 9月綜合PMI升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速 ，新訂單及就業亦
明顯成長 ，顯示消費 、AI投資與國防支出支撐景氣動能 。不過，需求快速擴張也加劇供應鏈
瓶頸，企業投入價格升至 2022年10月以來高點，燃料、運輸及原物料成本上升 ，顯示景氣韌
性與通膨壓力同步升溫，增加Fed政策維持緊縮的壓力。
美國
期中選舉
• 目前民調顯示 ，民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ，這將有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期 ，進
而帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑 ，對長天期美債形成支撐 。此外既有資料顯示 ，選後一年美股
平均報酬率達12.4%，主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到經濟成長、企業獲利及貨
幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。

越南
• 越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數 。估計此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流
入越南。資產管理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約 25億美元。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風 ，除了能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經濟成長 ，今年以來越南成為
美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成為中國商品流入的中轉國 ，美對越的301條款
調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。

美國景氣強勢擴張，通膨與利率壓力再升
• 企業活動創逾五年新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速擴張 ：S&P
Global美國9月綜合PMI初值升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高，
且連續四個月加速成長；服務業 PMI升至58.7（59個月高點）、
製造業PMI升至57.0（52個月高點），產出亦同步加速 ，加上
訂單與就業成長皆達近年最快 ，顯示內需、AI投資及國防支出
共同支撐企業活動。S&P Global依PMI調查推估經濟年成長約
5%，Q3則上看4%，反映短期景氣動能明顯增強。
• 供給受限推高成本 ，通膨壓力再升溫： 需求快速擴張同時推高
供應鏈與產能壓力 ，企業未完成訂單升至 2022年5月以來高點 ，
供應商交貨延遲亦明顯惡化 ，反映產能不足 ，投入價格指數升

至2022年10月以來新高 ，企業主要反映燃料 、運輸、原物料
及薪資成本上升 ，顯示供給受限與能源成本可能持續向終端價
格傳導，增加通膨黏著風險並提高Fed維持緊縮政策的壓力。
• 後市觀察：數據公布後，10年期美債殖利率一度升至約 5.12%，
創2007年以來新高 ，市場對10月再升息的押注亦升溫 ，短線
需關注供應鏈壓力是否緩解 ，以及後續ISM、就業與物價數據
能否確認通膨再升溫趨勢 。投資策略建議關注利率敏感度較低
的高品質成長股或防禦型資產 ，可適度布局受惠強勁內需與 AI
／國防支出的美股大型科技與工業類股 ，並留意升息環境下金
融股的利差受益機會 ，整體建議維持分散配置並嚴控利率風險 。
資料來源：S&P Global PMI, Bureau of Economic Analysis via S&P Global Market
Intelligence， 2026.9.24

能源成本升高推升通膨，利率維持高檔壓力升溫
• 能源與運輸成本上升，增加通膨黏著風險：中東衝突與
俄羅斯煉油設施遭攻擊 ，使全球柴油供應持續偏緊 ，燃
料價格及運輸成本同步上升；由於柴油廣泛應用於運輸 、
農業與製造業，價格上漲將透過物流及生產成本向下游
傳導。目前全球柴油庫存偏低 、煉油產能受限，若供應
中斷延續，可能使能源價格維持高檔並延長通膨壓力。
• 公債與企業債供給增加 ，長端利率壓力升高：美國財政
赤字及政府融資需求偏高 ，加上大型科技公司持續為 AI
資料中心等基礎建設籌資 ，債券供給增加使市場承接壓
力升高；在通膨預期未完全穩定的情況下 ，投資人對長
天期債券要求較高的期限溢酬 ，進一步推升長端殖利率 ，
同時AI相關企業債近期亦出現利差擴大 ，反映市場對大

量發債及資本支出回收期的關注升高。
• 資產配置面臨成長與通膨雙重拉扯： 能源價格、企業融
資成本及長端殖利率若持續處於高檔 ，將提高企業資金
成本並壓縮高估值資產的評價空間；同時 ，利率與能源
價格波動也可能擴大股 、債市震盪。因此資產配置建議
降低長天期債券對利率變化的敏感度 ，提高對通膨與利
率波動的防禦能力 ，並以現金流穩定、具備定價能力之
優質標的為核心。
資料來源：Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis via FRED ， 2026.9.8

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.9

美國期中選舉-目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數
• 目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ：最新民調顯示，民主黨
於11月美國期中選舉，取得國會多數席次的機率已領先共和黨，根據市場
情境分析，期中選舉不影響既有法案執行 ，但將左右未來兩年的財政預算 、
稅制改革 、政府支出及國會監督權限 。情境(1)若共和黨續掌國會 ，減稅
政策延長、產業回流、關稅及移民限制等議程有望加速推進 ，財政擴張力
道可能進一步增強 。情境(2)分裂國會 ，兩黨制衡將使重大法案推動難度
提高，新增減稅與大型財政刺激方案較難落地 。情境(3)民主黨全面掌控
國會，則可能提高對政府政策的監督力度 ，政策方向偏向提高稅負與監管 ，
壓抑企業獲利 ，使政策環境趨向平衡。

美國期中選舉-對於股債市的影響
• 對股市的影響：美國銀行分析1900至2025年共31次美國期中選舉，資料發現標普500在期中選舉前12個月平均報酬約為2.9%，
低於所有年度平均 8.9%。因為市場在選舉前往往受到政策不確定性 、政黨競爭及投資人觀望情緒影響 ，波動相對較大 。然而
此現象不代表選舉必然導致市場疲弱，因為當時的經濟環境、通膨、利率及企業獲利等因素同樣扮演重要角色。期中選舉後12
個月的股市表現通常較佳 。歷史數據顯示，選後一年美股平均報酬率達12.4%。主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到
經濟成長、企業獲利及貨幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。
• 對債市的影響：若民主黨取得國會主導權，市場普遍預期財政支出擴張幅度將較受節制 ，有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期，進而

帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑，對長天期美債形成支撐，若共和黨持續全面掌控國會，市場可能預期減稅及財政擴張政策延續，
進一步推升財政赤字預估，帶動公債殖利率走高，對長天期美債形成壓力，不過市場已部分反映相關預期，實際衝擊幅度有限。
美國期中選舉前後對於美股的影響
資料來源：美國銀行，2026.7
資料來源：復華投信，2026.9

AI建設熱潮吞噬自由現金流
• 資本支出創新高：雲端服務巨頭(包括亞馬遜、Alphabet、微軟、Meta、甲骨文以及SpaceX等)正在全力擴充AI算力與資料
中心，2026年大型雲端服務巨頭的資本支出合計預估將接近 9,600億美元，而2025至2031年的累計AI資本支出更可能逼近
9兆美元。
• 自由現金流出現赤字： 高額的投資加上股東回報(股息與庫藏股買回)，使許多巨頭的自由現金流(FCF)轉為負值(如Alphabet、
亞馬遜、微軟、Meta及甲骨文等)，預計到2027年，部分企業將更加依賴外部融資，而非單靠內部現金生成。
• 債券發行量大增： 為了填補資金缺口 ，科技業債券發行規模急遽上升 ，2026年迄今，各幣別的投資級科技債券發行量已突
破4,000億美元，創下歷史新高。

獲利韌性與金庫深度：強勁EBITDA成為信用安全網
• 上兆美元的流動性護城河： 儘管債務增加，但科技十二巨頭合計持有近 1兆美元的等值現金，整體科技板塊流動性充裕 ，短
期內無流動性危機。
• EBITDA強勁成長消化債務：市場預期覆蓋的12家最大科技企業在2026與2027年的EBITDA合計將成長約35%，強勁的獲利
能力顯著降低槓桿比率的上升壓力，確保信評機構維護穩定的信用評級。
• 資本配置策略分化：(1)兼顧股東回報：蘋果、微軟、Meta與Alphabet在投入AI同時，仍維持大規模的庫藏股買回與股息發
放。(2)全數投入資本支出：亞馬遜將絕大部分現金導向資本支出(2026年資本支出預計達2,200億美元居首)，且不發放股息。
• 小結與投資建議：科技巨頭為維持AI技術與基礎設施領先優勢，短期內，負自由現金流將成為常態。然而，這並非財務危機

訊號，而是企業利用超強獲利能力 (高EBITDA)與龐大的現金儲備進行戰略性擴張 ，科技板塊的信用風險目前處於可控且健
康的狀態，投資建議聚焦AI變現能力與EBITDA成長，優先佈局具有強大獲利護城河且能將算力轉化為實質營收的龍頭企業 。
科技十二巨頭的等值現金
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.23

越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數
• 預估引導60億美元資金流入越南： 富時羅素估計，此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流入越南 。受此提振，資產管
理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約25億美元。整個指數過渡將分為四個階段推行至2027年：
今年9月率先納入10%，明年3月追加20%，最後於明年6月與9月分別完成剩餘各35%的分配。市場分析指出，隨著明年3月
配額加大，市場關注度與對本地投資人的影響將更為顯著。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風： 儘管官方早前誓言今年 GDP成長目標達10%，但中東衝突以來能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經
濟成長。此外，越南與美國的貿易協商仍在進行中，由於今年以來越南成為美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成

為中國商品流入的中轉國，美對越的301條款調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。
• 觀點：儘管短線面臨不確定性 ，但越南股市同時具備多項利多題材 ，包含企業獲利成長、公共投資加速、FDI回流及資本市
場改革等。中長期結構仍偏正向，可以衛星配置的角度布局，或是以定期定額的方式參與長期成長潛力。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.09。

兆豐銀行所做任何投資意見與市場分析結果，係依據資料製作當時情況進行判斷

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部維持台達電買進投資建議，主係：(1) 3Q26受缺料影響出貨
遞延，並認列存貨跌價損失，但漲價效益將自4Q26起逐步反映，延
續至1Q27，毛利率可望逐步回升，營運最壞情況已過；(2)資料中
心相關營收占比已達五成，液冷今年占比約13%，全年營收可望突
破900億元，明年仍維持成長；(3) 缺電與缺料雖然影響資料中心
建置節奏，但管理層表示訂單能見度仍高，主要是出貨時點遞延，
需求並未消失；(4) 股價回檔幅度已大，評價已明顯回落。綜上所
述，研究部微幅下修27年營收1.04兆元(原估1.05兆元)，EPS下修
至60.37元(原估62.68元)。評價方面，考量HVDC滲透率仍不明朗，
僅給予31倍本益比，目標價由2,400元下修至1,870元。整體而言，
營運最壞情況已過且股價回檔已多，維持買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26受缺料干擾，毛利率承壓但OPM大致持穩 
3Q26營收1,971億元(原估2,102億元)， 季增7.5%， 年增31.1%，
營收達成率93.7%，低於預期，主因是資料中心客戶長短料、
建置延遲，造成出貨遞延。毛利率降至34.1%(原估35.4%)，季
減1.6pts， 低於預期 ， 主係因出貨遞延公司因此認列約39億元
存貨跌價損失(約影響1.7%，涉及產品非VR相關產品，研究部
推測為ASIC產品)，加上漲價在3Q26幾乎還沒反映。不過費用
控管得宜，營利率16.6%(原估16.9%)，僅季減0.1pts，本業獲
利能力大致持穩 。 業外收益受股市評價影響 ， 由2Q26的44億元
降至10億元，EPS為9.04元(原估10.54元)，低於預期。

2. 4Q26毛利率可望止跌回升，營運低點已過 
由於缺料尚未完全解除，研究部微幅調整4Q26營收至2,248億
元(原估2,253億元)，但季增14.1%、年增39.1%，成長動能仍
強。獲利方面，公司表示4Q毛利率優於3Q，主係存貨跌價損失
新增認列有望減少 、 開始有較多新價格體現， 以及電源業務成
長 帶 動 產 品 組 合 改 善 ， 故 研 究 部 預 估 毛 利 率34.8%( 原估
35.8%)， 季增0.7pts； 營利率17.2%(原估18.2%)， 季增0.6pts；
EPS下修至10.14元(原估11.03元)。展望1Q27，研究部預估營
收2,308億元，季增2.6%，表現優於以往的淡季季節性，主要
是部分AI遞延訂單可望延續至1Q27出貨。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. 資料中心營收占比過半，訂單能見度仍高 
管理層表示 ， 今年資料中心相關營收已占總營收一半 ，冷卻系
統約占13%，液冷3Q營收約250億元，26年營收可望突破900億
元 ， 且公司對1H27液冷需求看法仍偏樂觀 。雖然缺電影響部分
資料中心的建置進度 ，但管理層強調訂單能見度高， 變化主要
是出貨時點的延後，並非需求消失。此外，研究部認為，台達
電在供應鏈管理上具備優勢， 隨著缺料逐漸緩解 ，遞延的需求
可望陸續轉為營收。

4. 然Rubin Ultra機櫃出現低壓方案，HVDC滲透率仍待觀察 
HVDC方面 ， 正負400V進展較快 ， 已開始出貨 ；800V則由NV主推 ，
進度較慢。根據供應鏈調查，Ultra平台目前已有低壓設計方
案，若TDP增幅不大，HVDC的省電效益相對有限，客戶未必非
用800V不可，因此研究部下修2027年營收預估。此外，公司已
通過馬來西亞檳城新據點的投資案 ， 為中長期AI電源需求預先
擴充產能。在Vicor相關訴訟上，仍在ITC調查程序中，短期對
出貨不會有影響，公司對自身IP技術具有信心。

5. 評價已回落，維持買進 
綜上所述，研究部下修2026年營收至7,645億元(原估7,781億
元)，年增37.8%；毛利率35.3%(原估35.9%)；EPS下修至36.77
元(原估39.16元)。 同步下修2027年營收至1.04兆元(原估1.05
兆元)， 年增36.4%； 毛利率36.1%(原估36.5%)；EPS下修至60.37
元(原估62.68元)，年增64.2%。考量HVDC滲透率仍不明朗，僅
給予31倍本益比， 目標價由2,400元下修至1,870元 。 惟股價評
價已明顯回落，且營運最壞情況已過，維持買進建議。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上

### Page 2

New capacity in expansion: The company continues to expand its production scale to 
fulfill rising clients’ orders from LEO satellite and innovative business. We expect to see 
growing LEO satellite revenues contribution from 75% in 2025 to 97% in 2028E. 
Management notes the company has started the planning of next-stage expansion, and 
will start to expand capacity in Taiwan, Vietnam and US sites from 4Q26E and onwards 
to diversify the global production sites, improve the product delivery capabilities, and 
supply elasticity.

Earnings revision: We factor in UMT 3Q26 earnings and revise down earnings by 5%/ 
10%/ 9% in 2026-28E mainly on lower GM in 2026-28E by 3.8ppts~3.9ppts, reflecting 
longer time of transition to new LEO satellites platform, while we expect to see 
continuous GM improvement ahead on rising LEO satellite buisness carrying higher GM.

Valuation: We continue to derive our target price based on a discounted P/E, and based 
on 2029E (methodology unchanged). We continue to derive the target P/E multiple from 
the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the satellite supply chain, and we remove outliers 
(telecom operators who face competition from LEO satellite operators). Our target 
2029E discounted P/E multiple is at 35.1x (vs. previously at 37.1x) which is based on 
peers’ avg. PEG&M, and UMT’s 2029-30E avg. NI YoY and OPM, due to lower avg. OPM in 
2029-30E at 38% (vs. previously at 42%). We apply the 35.1x multiple to 2029E EPS and

### Page 4

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 35.1x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,188. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 30 Oct 2026 close.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this

### Page 1

Update
Nuvoton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Losses Widen; BMC Growth and
Japan Restructuring Remain Key
3Q losses widened Q/Q amid lower gross profit, increased opex
and reduced non-operating income. Management sees scope for
faster BMC growth in 2027, with execution and Japan
restructuring critical to delivering earnings growth.
3Q losses widened: Revenue was NT$7.86bn, -1% Q/Q, +8% Y/Y, 3% above our
estimate; gross profit was NT$2.9bn, -3% Q/Q, +13% Y/Y, 6% above our forecast;

gross margin was 37.4%, -0.6ppt Q/Q, +1.7ppt Y/Y, 1.2ppt above our estimate. Loss
per share was NT$1.24, wider Q/Q, Y/Y, and vs. our expectation. Management
attributed gross margin weakness to product mix; back-end supply constraints and
substantial AI-related R&D left operational progress around 1Q behind plan.
Demand remains mixed: Auto demand has stabilized after prior Y/Y declines, but
management sees no clear rebound. PC demand was significantly weaker in 2H26

than in 1H26 with higher content per PC vs. 2025 helping cushion the impact of
lower volumes. Memory cost increases have been passed through on memory-
bundled MPUs, although some customers began pulling back in 4Q26. MCU
repricing reflects foundry and OSAT costs. Most 2027 capacity has been secured but
cost pass-through and supply availability do not establish margin expansion.
BMC and Japan remain the key milestones: Management sees scope for 2027 BMC

Y/Y growth to exceed 2026's, with existing customers and multiple new customers,
including a CSP, contributing. New customer revenue should initially be small with
fuller contributions over one to two years. Current-gen products should dominate
2027 shipments; next-gen volumes may start modestly in 2H27. Japan remains the
source of losses, making the Tower JV restructuring targeted by management for
end-March 2027 central to recovery.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Ethan Jia
Research Associate

Earnings Estimate Revisions
We factor in the actual 3Q results: 3Q losses widened due to higher opex, lower non-
operating income and tax expenses. Uncertainties remain around BMC and Japan, and
demand remains mixed, so we do not yet revise our 2027-28 EPS estimates significantly.

Nuvoton: Valuation Methodology
PT is unchanged at NT$95.00: We factor in our earnings estimate changes. We maintain
the other assumptions in our RI model, including a 9.2% cost of equity (beta of 1.2, risk-
free rate of 2.0% and risk premium of 6.0%), payout ratio of 55%, a medium-term growth
rate of 7.0% and a terminal growth rate of 3.5%. Our bull- and bear-case scenario values
also are unchanged at NT$143.00 and NT$48.00,  respectively.

Risk Reward - Nuvoton Technology Corporation (4919.TW)
NT$95.00
Base case, residual income model. We assume a 9.2% cost of equity (beta 1.2, risk-free rate
2.0% and risk premium 6.0%), a payout ratio of 55%, a medium-term growth rate of 7.0%,
and a terminal growth rate of 3.5%.
▪ Demand in the auto and industrial
segments is recovering, but at a mild pace.
▪ Nuvoton is well positioned to expand MPU
share through bundled sales with Winbond's

DDR4.
▪ We also see business opportunities in
BMC/BMIC for AI servers.
▪ We expect the company to remain loss-
making in 4Q26 and are awaiting GM
recovery before turning more positive on
the stock, which we view as overvalued.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 160: 261104_daiwa_ Nien Made Enterprise

Source PDF: all report pdf/261104_daiwa_ Nien Made Enterprise.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: Nien Made reported its 3Q26 results on 4 November 2026 
after market hours. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call (see our previous flash, 
2Q26 results: record-high gross margin, 12 August 2026, for more 
information).

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results review: slight miss on higher opex. 
Nien Made’s 3Q26 revenue was TWD8,482m (+7.0% QoQ, +15.9% YoY), 
roughly in line with our estimate and the consensus. Its gross margin was 
60.8%, a record-high level, up from 60.0% in 2Q26 and 58.7% in 3Q25, 
beating our estimate (59.6%) and the consensus (59.8%). This was driven 
by price hikes in its custom-made segment and a lower tariff effect, despite 
being partially offset by restocking demand from ready-made products. 
However, its operating margin came in at 29.2%, up from 27.2% in 2Q26 
and the same as in 3Q25, lower than our estimate (30.3%) and the 
consensus (30.1%), due to surging fuel surcharges following oil price hikes 
in 3Q26. As such, operating profit was TWD2,473m (+14.5% QoQ, +15.9% 
YoY), missing our estimate and the consensus by 6.3% and 2.5%, 
respectively. Nien Made’s 3Q26 net profit stood at TWD1,870m (+34.5% 
QoQ, +9.6% YoY), missing our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus by 
8.9% and 4.2%, respectively. Its 3Q26 EPS came in at TWD6.38.

2027: several new initiatives in products and markets. Nien Made is on 
track to meet its 2026 target of growing its custom-made revenue in the US 
by mid-single-digit % YoY, custom-made revenue in non-US markets by 
high-single-digit % YoY and ready-made revenue by low-single-digit % YoY. 
Besides, Nien Made will continue to launch new products such as “tool-
free, drill-free” easy-install products and new blind programmes, and 
upgrade its motorised window coverings. Nien Made will continue to invest 
in its window covering brands, channels and service network (ie, European 
markets) as well as manufacturing, which should be the key factor to drive 
more order wins in the long run, in our view. We cut our 2026E EPS by 3%, 
after factoring in the 3Q26 results and keep our 2027-28E EPS almost 
unchanged.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call on Nien Made and 
raise our 12M TP to TWD520 (from TWD515), still based on a target PER 
of 20x on our 1-year forward EPS vs. its past 3-year PER trading range of 
11-21x. Key downside risks: lower-than-expected revenue from custom-
made products and macro/tariff uncertainties.

How we differ: We are more positive on the company’s leading position 
with share-gain potential. Nien Made is one of our preferred stocks in 
Taiwan’s non-tech sector.

4 November 2026

3Q26 results: record-high gross margin

➢ Despite record-high gross margin, 3Q26 net profit missed on opex 
➢ 2027: ongoing product mix optimisation trend  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; raising 12M TP to TWD520

Company profile 
Founded in July 2007 and listed on Taiwan’s main board in December 2015, Nien Made Enterprise 
(Nien Made) produces window coverings such as venetian blinds, shutters and shades (ie, cellular 
shades, roller blinds and roman blinds). Its major clients include dealers, designers and small specialty 
stores as well as big box retailers (ie, Home Depot and Wal-Mart).

### Page 5

5

Nien Made Enterprise (8464 TT): 4 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 24 Jun 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Its CEO and Chairman is the same person. Also, 3 brothers, with 1 being the CEO (Chairman)
and 2 being Vice President, are in charge of different divisions, managing and collaborating
collectively, which we view as a positive sign for solid corporate governance.
Capital management 2
Nien Made's dividend payout ratio was c.80% before 2019, but dropped to 52-71% from 2019 to
2025, which we attribute to its capacity expansion plans in Mexico and some adjustment for
COVID-19.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related-party transactions stood at c.60% of total revenue over 2022-25, with
majority from Norman International Inc. and its subsidiary. We see no evidence of suspicious
activities.
E Product design & lifecycle management 1
We note Nien Made has improved its quality, safety and efficiency throughout its product
lifecycle. The company has developed its own B2B platform for its customers for a better user
experience and efficiency. Nien Made's products have a lifespan ranging from 10 to 20 years.
Therefore, in addition to controlling production conditions through equipment such as drying kilns
and controlled-environment storage, Nien Made also focus on mitigating consumers’ health and
safety risks.
S Supply chain management 2
Nien Made's overall local-to-local raw material sourcing was at 71% in 2023, which we believe
will help reduce transportation costs in the long term. Moreover, Nien Made established supplier
management policies and relevant regulations, and its 234 suppliers signed the Supplier Code of
Conduct as of 2023.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 3

NYPCB (8046.TW)
05 November 2026 Citi Research
3
NYPCB
Valuation
Our DCF-based TP for NYPCB is NT$1,700. With a risk-free rate of 5.00% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk
premium of 7% and an equity beta of 1.22 (YTD weekly share price), we calculate NYPCB's WACC as 13.4%. Our TP is equivalent
to 30x/13x 2027E EPS/BVPS, which reflects its high P/E multiple during upcycles. NYPCB traded at 15-20x P/E during its
2003-2008 upcycle driven by PC/NB demand strength. In 2010-11 when the market was recovering from the global financial

crisis, NYPCB traded at 25-35x P/E. In the last 2019-22 upcycle when ABF was in shortage, NYPCB traded at 30-40x P/E.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the NYPCB shares from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected ABF
demand from key customers; 2) BT business seasonality weakness and fewer-than-expected price hikes; and 3) severe T glass
shortages that cause a bottleneck to its production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

3Q26 EPS 16.9 元，優於預期 3 成 
3Q26 EPS  16.95 元，優於市場預期約 3 成，最大差異在於營收季增 35%，
優於 teens 季增之目標，主要動能來自 CPU 需求強勁、新增 Inference 專案
量產及獲得台積產能支持。本季毛利率季減 1.0 個百分點達 20.6%，主要受
到產品組合因素。以 3Q26 主要製程別變化，包括 N3 佔比由上季 51%升至
55%、N5 由 5%升至 12%，主要反映 CSP CPU 拉貨動能強勁。 
4Q26 CPU 續強、non CPU 新量產，營收達全年高峰 
展望 4Q26 為傳統入帳高峰及延續 CPU 需求強勁、MSFT Maia 及中國車用
專案放量，營收為全年高峰，根據先前假設全年 NRE 年增近持平、 4Q26 
Turnkey 佔比近 9 成，推估 4Q26 營收季增 1~2 成及 26 全年營收年增近 1
倍。毛利率受到產品結構因素，估計持平至略降、費用持平至略增，推估
EPS 18.61 元/季增 9.8%。 
COT 分工模式、公司 APT 競爭優勢，中長期展望維持樂觀 
2026 年營收成長來自 CSP N3 CPU ，估計營收佔比>5 成。AI 擴大至推論、
Agentic 應用及 CSP 由傳統晶片統包轉向 COT 分工模式，加上產能獲得支
援，使得 公司 27~28 年展望較先前樂觀，主要成長來自 CPU 需求強勁且含
蓋至少 2~3 家 CPU 客戶。2H27 美系車用客戶 AI 專案放量、MSFT Maia 及
其他 inference 專案為潛在動能，而 Google TPU 、Meta 等潛在效益落在 29
年 。 元 大 最 新 預 估 2026/27/28 EPS 59.5/142.1 /208.4 元 ， 分 別 上 修
9%/26%/28%，係因上修 27/28 年 CPU 出貨量及 Maia 營收貢獻，除了
CPU 佔比>4 成，Maia 二年佔比約 18%/22%，毛利率降 1 個百分點、費用
假設略降。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 EPS  16.95 元，優於預期約 3 成。預期 4Q26 CPU 需求強勁、
新增 inference 專案、MSFT Maia 系列及中國車用專案放量，營運展
望樂觀，估計 2026 全年營收 YoY 接近倍數成長。  
⧫  
隨著 AI 擴大至推論/代理型應用，帶動 CPU 需求爆發及營運上修，延
續至 27~28 年接續美系車用專案量產、MSFT，29 年來自 CSP 轉向
COT 效益推升營運達另一高峰。 
⧫  
預估 2026/27/28 年 EPS 59/142/208 元，上修 9%/26%/28%，維持買
進，目標本益比降至 45 倍、2028 年 EPS，推得目標價 9360 元。近
期 AI 族群受系統風險、籌碼面因素而使評價面轉趨保守。然看好中長
期 Agentic AI、COT 趨勢明確，帶動公司營運更具爆發力。

主要動能來自 CPU 需求強勁、新增 Inference 專案量產及獲得台積產能支持。本季毛利率季減 1.0
個百分點達 20.6%，主要受到產品組合因素。以 3Q26 主要製程別變化，包括 N3 佔比由上季 51%

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：IC 設計 2026 年 11 月 2 日 Global Unichip 第3 頁，共 14頁  
Agentic AI  帶動 CPU 價值重估、公司營運上修 
隨 AI 大型模型成熟漸漸由訓練轉向 Inference/agentic AI ，預期全球 Token 需求每三個月翻倍，其
中 ASIC 可針對客戶需求打造客製化晶片，算力使用效率較 GPU 更貼近客戶需求、較佳的性價比。
全球 AI ASIC 市場 25~28 年 CAGR 達 35~40%，估計 ASIC 滲透率將突 4 成。Agentic AI 在於主
動任務執行，相較於過往 LLM 矩陣運算，強調 CPU/GPU/ASIC 的協同運算需求，帶動 CPU 搭配
比例提升、創意營運上修空間。 
CSP 朝 COT 模式，帶動更多 IC 設計服務委外的商機 
CSP 由傳統晶片廠統包朝向 COT(Customer Owned Tooling) 分工模式，轉由 CSP 主導晶片架構與
前端設計流程，將資源集中於最具價值的 Architecture/RTL 前端設計，將實體設計、先進封裝、
chiplet 後端設計服務分拆外包，基於晶片複雜度提升及開發成本墊高，藉由分工達分散供應鏈風險，
對傳統 ASIC 廠商造成價值鏈瓜分，對 backend 委外需求增加反而有利於純設計服務廠商。創意布
局包括 HBM2/3/3E/4/4E、GLink 相關 IP、CoWoS/SOIC/DoD 解決方案，自有 IP 具客製化彈性，
在 TSMC APT 生態系的重要提升，承接底層設計機會大增，為 COT 模式下的主要受惠者之一。 
COT 分工模式、公司 APT 競爭優勢，中長期展望維持樂觀 
2026 年營收成長來自 CSP N3 CPU ，估計營收佔比>5 成。AI 擴大至推論、Agentic 應用及 CSP
由傳統晶片統包轉向 COT 分工模式，加上產能獲得支援，使得 公司 27~28 年展望較先前樂觀，主
要成長來自 CPU 需求強勁且含蓋至少 2~3 家 CPU 客戶。2H27 美系車用客戶 AI 專案放量、MSFT 
Maia 及其他 inference 專案為潛在動能，而 Google TPU 、Meta 等潛在效益落在 29 年。元大最新
預估 2026/27/28 EPS 59.5/142.1/208.4 元，分別上修 9%/26%/28%，係因上修 27/28 年 CPU 出
貨量及 Maia 營收貢獻，除了 CPU 佔比>4 成，Maia 二年佔比約 18%/22%，毛利率降 1 個百分點、
費用假設略降。

Agentic AI 將帶動 CPU 需求大幅提升 
Agentic AI 不再只是單次 prompt-response，需要反覆進行任務規劃、推理、檢查與修正。相較傳
統生成式 AI，工作流程更長、步驟更多，對系統層級調度能力要求大幅提升。 CPU 在 Agentic AI 
中主要負責 orchestration、資料前後處理，以及外部工具與資料源調用。包含  Web、Database、
API 等任務管理，協助 GPU / ASIC 專注於模型運算。隨 agent 工作流變長，CPU 與 GPU 的協同
效率將影響整體延遲、吞吐量，成為未來 AI server 架構升級重點。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：IC 設計 2026 年 11 月 2 日 Global Unichip 第4 頁，共 14頁  
圖 5：Agentic AI 的執行流程需要反覆的調用 CPU 與 GPU 的運算資源

資料來源：Semianalysis、元大投顧

解決推論延遲需提高 CPU 用量並優化演算法 
代理式 AI 涉及大量任務分解、環境切換、API 互動與外部工具調用，相關 orchestration latency 可
占總延遲約 50%-90%。搜尋網頁、分析內容、執行  Python 腳本與查詢資料庫等非模型推論任務，
多由 CPU 負責執行，因此增加 CPU 資源可直接緩解瓶頸。透過 COMB (CPU 感知微批次處理) 與 
MAS 可優化 CPU-GPU 協同演算法：COMB 將大批次任務拆分，使 CPU 與 GPU 可更同步執行，
研究顯示可提升約 1.8 倍運算效率。MAS 則依任務屬性區分 CPU-heavy 與 GPU-heavy workload，
建立彈性資源調度，避免任務壟斷整體運算資源。 
圖 6：當模型執行更複雜任務需要更多 CPU

資料來源：Semianalysis、元大投顧

不同的代理 AI 場景，CPU:GPU 之比例約落在 0.8-1.4:1 
代理式 AI 的 CPU 需求取決於工作流中工具調用與資料處理比例。以網頁資料蒐集為例， DOM 
parsing、摘要生成、網路 I/O 與資料整理多由 CPU 負責，CPU 延遲占比較高。因此若應用場景偏
向資料蒐集、網頁解析與外部工具互動， CPU:GPU 最適比例將提高至約  1.4:1，以降低整體推論
瓶頸。 
Excel 自動化、 Python / Bash 腳本執行與自動生成簡報，同樣需要大量  CPU 參與。相關任務
CPU 延遲占比約 25%-65%。因此，自動化與文件生成的 agent workflow，也可將 CPU:GPU 比例
提高至約 0.8:1，以改善整體任務效率。

產業概況  
IC 設計服務商透過新接案及轉量產來創造獲利，中長期受惠 ASIC 委外開發趨勢 
隨著半導體產業對晶片功能和性能要求增加，晶片結構日漸複雜、造價亦越來越昂貴，使得 IC 設
計工作困難度日益提高，進而成為 IC 設計服務業者重要之商機與切入點。IC 設計服務商可從最初
的產品概念、規格制定、開發、驗證、製造、生產到最終成品等各階段中協助下游客戶開發晶片，
客戶可彈性地選擇在任何環節切入半導體設計的產業鏈，並由設計服務業者完成後續統包作業。  
IC 設計服務商之營運以替客戶代為設計 IC 為主，其本身並沒有產品，整體業務通常可分為委託設
計(NRE，Non-Recurring Engineering) 及量產服務(Turnkey)，NRE 主要透過提供設計廠各種矽智
財(IP)及設計元件資料庫以接案開發產品，並收取一次性 NRE 費用來營利；而透過 NRE 委外設計
的 IC 晶片，經過 1-2 年設計開發完成後，若客戶本身並不具備與晶圓代工、封測等廠商業務往來
的經驗，但又想爭取有競爭力的投片價格與優異的技術服務，便會進一步透過和 IC 設計服務商代
為投片量產，以達成大量生產之需求，這部分業務則稱為 Turnkey。 
一般而言，NRE、Turnkey 之毛利率分別約落在 30%-40%、10%-20%，然根據不同案件、客戶、
規模、製程、應用、提供服務之數量等，仍會使利潤略有所差異。此外，儘管 Turnkey 毛利率較低，
但由於案件已進入到量產階段，IC 設計服務商僅需耗費少部分營運量能便可帶來可觀的營收貢獻，
故 NRE 案件最終是否能順利轉 Turnkey 亦將成為廠商在接案上的重要考量因素。 
隨 5G/AI/HPC 需求於近年激增及晶圓代工廠持續往更先進製程邁進，未來將會有越來越多的系統 /
品牌廠出現差異化晶片或自行研發晶片的需求，研調機構 Global Information 指出，全球 ASIC 晶
片市場將於 2030 年達到 355 億美元，2023~2030 年 CAGR 可望達 8.2%，中長期而言 IC 設計服
務產業有望受惠半導體產業大勢。

圖 8：設計服務商負責設計後之統包服務 圖 9：營運模式以 NRE 及 Turnkey 為主

獲利調整與股票評價  
維持買進，2028 年目標本益比降至 45 倍，推得目標價 9360 元 
3Q26 EPS  16.9 元，優於預期，主要差異在於 CPU 出貨優於預期及新增 inference 訂單，使得營
收季增 35%，優於原先預估 teens 成長，毛利率落在 20.6%則符合預期。展望 4Q26 延續 CPU 動
能更為強勁，加上台積產能支持，預估 2026 全年營收 YoY 有機會成長近 1 倍。中長期產業趨勢包
括 AI 應用擴大、ASIC 滲透率提升及委外趨勢及先進封裝重要性提升，CSP 策略由傳統晶片廠由
統包轉向 COT 分包方式，公司策略則以台積電先進封裝及後段實體設計為競爭優勢，承接底層設
計，提供更好的成本結構，可望推升營運達另一高峰。2026~27 年主要成長動能來自 CPU，隨 AI
擴大至推論或代理 AI，CPU 用量持續上修，北美車用 AI 專案預計 2Q27 量產，進入新一輪成長週
期，另外，MSFT Maia 、新增 inference 專案為 27~28 年潛在動能，COT 模式對公司 29 年訂單展
望 更 加 樂 觀 ， 包 括 Google TPU 、 Mata 等 。 元 大 最 新 預 估 2026/27/28 年 EPS 分 別 為
59.5/142.1/208.4 元，其中 follow 26/27/28 年 TPU 預估量分別>4M/>7M/>8Ｍ，CPU/TPU 搭配比
例提升至 1:2，相當於 CPU 出貨量較先前預估增加接近 1 倍，此外，公司 CPU 除了 Google，還
包括 MSFT 等 2~3 家、供應鏈產能獲得舒解，因而調高 CPU 預估，另外，調高來自 MSFT Maia
系列營收貢獻預估，估計 27/28 年分別為 18%/22%，毛利率估計降 1 個百分點，主要 CPU 佔比略
升。目前以 2026/27/28 年 EPS 換算，本益比分別為 129/54/37 倍。以 28 年評價基礎落在近一年
本益比區間 30-50 倍中下緣，約與國內同業平均本益比約 40.9 倍相當、國外同業平均本益比的
30.5 倍。隨著 CPU 規模上修、non CPU 包括 MSFT Maia 、車用等進入量產，公司營運進入更強
勁、更長遠的成長週期。維持買進，惟目標本益比由 50 倍降至 45 倍，主要反映近期半導體/AI 族
群受系統風險、籌碼面因素，使投資氛圍轉趨保守、評價面有所下修，以 28 年 EPS 評價基礎，推
得目標價 9360 元，然 AI ASIC 專案轉量產推升公司 2027~28 年營運成長趨勢明確，反映 Agentic、
COT 趨勢，有利公司未來發展並且帶動營運進入更強勁成長週期，基本面看法不變。

公司簡介  
創意電子(GUC)是先進 ASIC 領導廠商，主要提供全面 ASIC 設計服務，包括 Spec-in 和系統單晶片整合(SoC Integration) 、
實體設計(Physical Design)、先進封裝技術(Advanced Packaging Technology) 、量產服務以及 HBM 和 die-to-die 互連 IP。
客戶涵蓋人工智慧、高速運算、5G /網路、固態硬碟和工業領域等市場，據點遍及北美、中國、歐洲、韓國和日本等地。

### Page 11

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：IC 設計 2026 年 11 月 2 日 Global Unichip 第11 頁，共 14頁

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 創意整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排行在領先的位置，亦於
半導體設計與製造行業的公司中排名領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 創意的整體曝險較低，遠優於半導體設計與製造行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  ESG 議
題包含營運方面的人力資本管理、資源使用以及商業道德等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：創意在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。主因公司的揭露程度佳，表
明對投資者及公眾的強烈責任感，同時公司於 ESG 亦有相關舉措(節電、綠色生產降低產品功耗)，但尚不能藉此彌平所
有風險。儘管如此，創意並未涉及任何與 ESG 相關的重大爭議。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

주가 추이

3Q26 매 출액 과 영 업 이익 은 비현 금 성 비용 항목 개 선 으로 시장 기대치
를 상 회 했 으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인 플 레 이션 등으로 연간 가이 던 스 는 
하 향 되 었 습니 다. 올해 생산 차질 에 따라 이 연된 신차 판 매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시 화 하 는 부분 에 주목 필 요 합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 Y oY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제 외 하 면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년 으 로 이연된 것 이 라 는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본 격 화 된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로 컬 업 체 들 의 공세를 방 어 하 는 모 습 을 
확인하는 것이 가 능 할 것으로 판 단 한 다. 노 후 차 종 중심 운 영 에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근 원 적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 30일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계

Document 268: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

### Page 1

4 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.

We have calculated the year-on-year change in the number of new business start-ups. The table categorises these results as positive (red), neutral (white) or negative (blue). The darker
the colour, the larger the change. The data series are defined as follows: Germany = new entries in the companies register (start-ups) since 2008; France = business start-ups, including

micro-entrepreneurs, since 2000; United Kingdom = businesses registered for VAT/PAYE since 2017; United States = business applications (tax identification number) since 2004. Sectors
have been grouped together to ensure that the classification is standardised across all countries. For the third quarter, the German data only include the months of July and August.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

AI バブル測定器としての鉱工業生産（26 年９月） 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・筆者が日本経済と株式市場の行方を読む上で定点観測している日本の鉱工業生産は、2026年
９月に前月比＋2.2％となり、市場予想（同＋1.3％）を上回った。経産産業省の予測補正値
（同＋1.6％）に対しても上振れた。増産は３ヶ月ぶり。８月が速報段階の同▲1.7％から確
報で同▲2.4％へと下方修正されていたため、９月の増産で前月までの落ち込みを概ね取り戻
した格好となる。もっとも、生産水準は直近最高だった６月の104.6を上回るには至っておら
ず、数量ベースの生産活動は、経済産業省の判断通り「一進一退」となっている。

・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPYは先行き12ヶ月 155円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12月に 1.5％とした後、27年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FEDはFF金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

2/5 
・増産となったのは８業種。上位３業種は自動車工業、生産用機械工業、汎用・業務機械工業
であった。反対に減産の寄与度が大きかった３業種は鉄鋼・非鉄金属、窯業・土石製品工
業、化学工業（除く無機・有機化学工業・医薬品）であった。７月に熊本で発生した地震に
よって８月までは自動車生産などに影響が出ていたが、その挽回生産が９月の数値を押し上
げる格好となった。

・10月初旬に実施された生産予測調査に基づけば、製造工業の生産計画は10月に前月比＋5.1％
となった後、11月は同＋0.8％と増産が見込まれている。経産省経済解析室が生産予測指数の
上振れバイアスを補正した10月の予測値は同＋3.9％とかなり強い。電子部品・デバイス工業
と生産用機械工業で大幅な増産が見込まれているほか、自動車の挽回生産も続く見込みであ
る。生産活動全体の方向感を決める輸送機械工業は９月（実績）に前月比＋5.5％となった
後、10月（計画）に同＋0.9％、11月に同＋4.3％となっている。日本や米国における自動車
販売台数が堅調に推移していることを踏まえると、基調的に弱含んでいく展開は想定しにく
い。また生産活動の先行指標として有用な製造業ＰＭＩが４月以降に有意な上昇を示してい
ることから判断すると、製造業全体としてはＡＩ・半導体需要が浸透する形で増勢が強まっ
ていくと期待される。10月製造業ＰＭＩは53.4と高水準を維持している。

・なお、鉱工業生産指数は生産の「数量」に重きを置いて生産高を計測する統計であり、「付
加価値」を直接捕捉する性格のものではない。例えば、高付加価値を狙った品質向上によっ
て生産数量・重量が減少する場合、統計上はそれを減産と見做し、付加価値を過小評価して
しまうこともある。したがって、鉱工業生産統計が示すほど日本経済（製造業）の付加価値

創出力が停滞していない可能性を念頭に置く必要がある。事実、過去数年は付加価値の合計
である実質ＧＤＰと鉱工業生産指数の水準・方向感の乖離が大きくなっている。

・株式市場における重要度が高い電子部品・デバイス工業については、前月比＋3.6％の増産で
あった。地震の影響もあってか、ＩＣの増産に一服感がみられる一方、コンデンサや電子回
路基板などで増産の動きがみられる。生産用機械工業に分類される半導体製造装置は同＋
3.4％の増産となり、３ヶ月平均値でみても＋7.7％と力強い増産基調にある。生産予測調査
に目を向けると、電子部品・デバイス工業は10月に同＋16.6％、11月に同▲1.7％と均してみ
れば大幅な増産が計画されている。半導体製造装置が含まれる生産用機械工業は10月に同＋
15.5％と大幅増産となった後、11月は同▲5.7％と減産が見込まれているが、こちらも基調は
強い。個別企業の業績予想などから判断しても、先行きは半導体製造装置の増産がかなりの
高確度で期待できる。関連指標の機械受注統計における「電子計算機等」の受注額水準も鈍
化する気配に乏しい。この指標は日経平均株価との連動性がある。

・電子部品・デバイス工業の動向を仔細にみると、９月は出荷が前年比＋0.8％へと減速するな
か、在庫は同▲6.1％へとマイナス幅を拡大した。この結果、出荷・在庫バランス（両者の前
年比差分から算出）は＋6.9pとなり、３ヶ月平均でも＋7.0ptとはっきりとしたプラス領域を
維持した。引き続き、製品需給が引き締まる方向にあることが示唆されている。ここで電子
部品・デバイス工業の出荷・在庫バランスと、日経平均株価が長期的に連動性を有してきた
事実を再確認したい。株価指数において半導体を直接手掛ける企業の存在感は必ずしも大き
くはないものの、半導体製造装置、電子部品、化学品など「広義半導体」で見ればその存在
感は大きく、結果的に日本株全体のうねりを作り出す構図があると筆者は理解している。ち
なみに出荷在庫バランス株価は方向感こそ一致しているものの、両者のかい離は大きくなっ
ている。その要因として、数量ベースの出荷・在庫バランスには、メモリなど製品価格の高
騰が反映されないことが考えられる。

Document 320: COP31 Preview_ Cooperation Amid Fragmentation (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/COP31 Preview_ Cooperation Amid Fragmentation (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 22
Status: ok

### Page 1

COP31 Preview: Cooperation Amid Fragmentation
COP31, taking place in Antalya, Türkiye from 9 to 20 November 2026, will test whether the ‘Implementation COP’ can
turn climate ambition into finance delivery amid heightened geopolitical tensions. Corporates and investors should
watch for signals that could shape policy and capital allocation, including commitments to grid investment,
electrification, adaptation and carbon markets. We also expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  | T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  | O C T O B E R  2 0 2 6
The report outlines the top 10 focus topics, discussions and outcomes for COP31 (see pages 1-3 for a summary)

### Page 2

C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
2 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
From 9 to 20 November, ~100,000 participants and nearly 200 Parties are expected at COP31 in Antalya, Türkiye. This is an ‘Implementation COP’ and will focus on climate finance delivery for
mitigation and adaptation commitments. Yes, there will be geopolitical tensions and competitiveness concerns. But, for corporates and investors, the discussions will shape how private capital is

mobilised, and how policies will affect de-risking efforts. We expect electrification efforts will feature prominently, driven by energy security concerns as well as by rising AI-driven power
demand. Furthermore, AI’s ability to help tackle climate change has the potential to play a bigger role in shaping the agenda of future COPs.
01
TÜRKIYE HOSTS, AUSTRALIA LEADS
NEGOTIATIONS
Türkiye and Australia must stay aligned
to drive progress
See page 6
– As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda, which is intended to build cooperation between

willing participants to drive climate action. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations that will seek consensus (or
lack of objection) on finance delivery and existing climate commitments. Feedback following pre-COP meetings on the
arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any potential disagreement will be handled.
– This structure is the result of a compromise after both countries bid to host. Both countries want COP31 to focus on climate

finance implementation and clean-energy growth, however, they remain coal-reliant (in different ways) with no national coal
phase-out date, a criticism likely to continue from climate advocates at COP.
02
CLIMATE FINANCE
Success at COP31 requires notable
progress on mobilising existing
commitments
See page 7
– For COP31 to be successful it will need to deliver agreements to mobilise and channel climate finance. For context, at COP29 in
2024 it was agreed (as part of formal negotiations) that developed nations would commit $300bn in climate finance annually

by 2035, with a call for $1.3tn from all sources. At present, more credible plans are needed to secure existing commitments
and attract broader capital. Note the EU has already stated it will not make new finance pledges at COP31.
– Article 9 contains the main financing provisions of the Paris Agreement and has driven much debate during COP talks.
Developing countries want focus on public funding from developed governments (Article 9.1), while developed countries prefer

to discuss Article 9 in a broader context. This allows private capital to be included. COP30 launched a two-year work
programme on Article 9, but June’s Bonn talks stalled, so COP31 meetings will attempt to reach agreement.
03
ADAPTATION & RESILIENCE
Implications of a 1.5°C overshoot point
to more adaptation financing
See pages 8–9
– The UN has warned that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable, at least temporarily. That has

dampened morale for climate supporters ahead of COP but, at the same time, it has drawn more attention to the topic of
adaptation finance, which is set to be on the formal negotiating agenda. Parties appear deadlocked over whether the COP30
goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 (potential annual target of $120bn) should be explicitly referenced in the final COP31
decision on the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA). This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal finance flows.

– The EU Commission proposed the Climate Resilience law on 27 October. It needs Parliament and Council approval, but if
approved, member states will be required to run national climate-risk assessments based on a 3°C-by-2100 warming scenario
(i.e. the EU is planning for overshoot). Members would also need to adopt resilience plans, including details of costs and
funding, with The Commission estimating the EU needs €70bn a year for adaptation to 2050, vs. €29bn of current spend.

### Page 3

Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
3 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
04
ELECTRIFICATION
Türkiye to champion voluntary
electrification commitments
See page 10
– Türkiye's voluntary Action Agenda includes a ‘35-by-35’ target to lift electricity’s share of final energy use from ~23% to 35% by
2035, alongside waste, building energy intensity and circularity targets. This sits outside the formal negotiations and commits
only those that volunteer. China and the EU have voiced support, and the timing is right given global energy-security concerns,

though some question the emissions impact if fossil fuels are used to generate the additional electricity.
06
TRADE TENSIONS
Trade’s inclusion in the provisional
agenda is contentious, with the
potential for disagreement over trade to
stall progress on wider agreements
See page 12
– The role of trade at COP is contentious, but China wants it discussed. On 21 October, Vice Minister Li Gao called ‘unilateralism
and protectionism’ a ‘new problem’ undermining collective climate efforts. Specifically, he wants COP31 to discuss the free

flow of green technology. This was widely read as being aimed at US and EU tariffs on Chinese cleantech.
– Despite a COP30 agreement to create three annual trade dialogues in 2026–28 (the first formal space under the UNFCCC to
discuss trade) away from COP, developing countries are still pushing for trade to be a formal negotiating item in Antalya, and it
is on the provisional agenda. They argue that climate-related trade measures such as the CBAM burden their exporters and

question whether they are compatible with UNFCCC principles. The EU meanwhile claims they help prevent carbon leakage,
with developed countries preferring trade discussions to focus on technical information-sharing without duplicating the WTO.
C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
GEOPOLITICS & MULTILATERALISM
Consensus under strain, but ‘coalitions
of the willing’ are rising
See pages 13–14
– Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus, historically central to COP decision-

making, harder among nearly 200 nations. This has encouraged more voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing” to pursue
more ambitious goals. If these strategies deliver more effective progress, their approach will shape future COPs.
05
FOSSIL FUELS
Phase-out likely not on the formal
agenda, but workarounds are emerging
See page 11
– Brazil’s COP30 presidency is to present a fossil fuel transition roadmap separately, outside the UN formal negotiating process.

For perspective, COP28 in Dubai reached consensus on “transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems”, but at COP30
in Belém (2025) calls from more than 80 countries for a transition roadmap faced opposition from other nations, so it was
omitted from the final decision and moved to a separate track.
– Santa Marta is a second workaround for the lack of formal agreement on a transition roadmap. The Colombia- and
Netherlands-led process is a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 participants (excluding the US, China, India and Russia) agreeing to

voluntary action to move away from fossil fuels faster than consensus allows. At COP31, Colombia and the Netherlands want
to bring the Santa Marta outcomes into the summit. The format is not yet settled, but the Australian and Turkish presidencies
have said they are open to it. The process then heads to a second conference in Tuvalu, co-hosted with Ireland, in 2027.

### Page 4

09
NATURE & CARBON CREDITS
Efforts to boost demand and build
credibility to help scale carbon markets
See page 16
– Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the
formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale, the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting
emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks,

however; we expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits.
– The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30, plans a fixed-income portfolio to reward verified forest
conservation. It now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target. In our view, fresh COP31 commitments
would signal appetite to fund nature conservation and protection.
10
PACIFIC, OCEANS & SEA-LEVEL RISE
Australia to bring Pacific priorities into

focus
See page 17
– Australia asked for a Pacific pre-COP in return for ceding the hosting. It has promised to keep championing Pacific interests at
COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration was adopted at that pre-COP on 6 October. ‘Taku Pakasoa’ is Tuvaluan for strength
found in unity and collective action. It is a joint call to keep 1.5°C alive, calling for action on climate finance, resilience and
oceans. Its intention is to give the Pacific a shared position going into COP31 and signal its policy priorities to other blocs.

Other countries are invited to sign on. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a COP31 focus, including investable solutions.
COP31 should indicate what COP can deliver in a more fragmented geopolitical world. Key questions? Can the current structure help mobilise meaningful finance and investment, and do
smaller coalitions and voluntary commitments deliver meaningful outcomes? Answers here could shape future COP agendas.  Looking ahead, Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027. There, we

expect a continued focus on delivering on commitments and improving access to climate finance, reflecting African priorities. COP33 then falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China
reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested.
We should note that the second Global Stocktake, the Paris Agreement’s five-yearly review of collective progress, begins at COP31 and concludes at COP33, with a technical assessment
(2027) informing the final negotiations. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

08
AI & CLIMATE
AI’s power demand in focus, with the
Antalya pledge calling for low emissions
infrastructure
See page 15
– The Antalya AI Pledge invites governments and companies to commit voluntarily to sustainable infrastructure and
environmental transparency. Its call for zero- and low-emissions energy infrastructure leaves room for nuclear power,
prompting discussion of its role in meeting data-centre demand alongside renewables. While we see binding commitments as

unlikely, watch for corporate power commitments and technology partnerships.
– We expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to feature prominently at COP31, prompting debate over data-
centre energy use. We also expect AI to help delegations manage COP’s heavy paperwork, from reviewing negotiating texts
and submissions to comparing successive draft decisions. This should help negotiating teams prepare more effectively and
make decisions faster, particularly smaller delegations with limited staff and resources.

### Page 6

TO P I C  1 | COP31’s UNIQUE STRUCTURE
COP31’s unique dual structure will need to bridge divides among negotiating groups and build consensus
As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations. This dual structure is the result of a compromise after
both countries bid to host. Feedback post pre-COP meetings on the unique arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any disagreement between the two countries

would be handled should it occur. Both countries clearly want this year’s COP to focus on climate finance implementation and clean-energy growth.
A unique leadership structure
COP31 will take place in Antalya, Türkiye. The setup is unusual, emerging as part of a deal
between Türkiye and Australia after both countries bid to host the event. What began as a
compromise could become an advantage, provided the two countries remain aligned. Their
differing diplomatic relationships could help them bridge divides between negotiating groups

and build common ground on climate action and finance.
How it will work
Murat Kurum, Türkiye’s Minister of the Environment, will serve as COP31 President, overseeing
the conference and Action Agenda. Australia’s Minister for Climate Change and Energy, Chris
Bowen, will serve as President of Negotiations, including drafting final texts, with authority to
steer government-to-government talks. Pre-COP preparations have reportedly been positive.
They will, however, both likely face criticism from climate advocates asking for a plan to shift

from fossil fuels, notably coal, given efforts to position themselves as global climate leaders.
The Action Agenda includes targets for electrification, waste, buildings and circularity
This voluntary Agenda proposes 2035 targets of a 35% electricity share of final energy use (up
from 23% currently), halving projected municipal waste growth, cutting building energy
intensity by at least 25% and reaching at least 15% circular material use. While pledges are

non-binding, they can signal policy and decisions that could support infrastructure investment.
Formal negotiations to focus on implementing existing commitments
Formal negotiations seek consensus (or lack of objection) on implementation pathways. The
key need is guidance on how agreed climate-finance commitments will be funded and
delivered, and on countries’ roles. Adaptation will also be a focus, with countries aiming to
reach agreement on how to advance the Global Goal on Adaptation after discussions stalled

earlier this year. These formal talks remain separate from the voluntary Action Agenda.
Sources: COP31 Action Agenda, IEA and Eco-Business
6

### Page 7

TO P I C  2 | C L I MATE F I N AN C E
Climate finance delivery will be a central test for COP31
June’s Bonn preparatory talks reportedly made little progress and exposed persistent finance divisions, leaving COP31 to turn both the $300bn and $1.3tn-by-2035 goals into a more credible
delivery plan. This is a big ask and requires clearer public/private roles, consistent tracking mechanisms, more concessional finance, risk-sharing and guarantees that can help reduce the cost of

capital. New large-scale finance commitments appear unlikely, with the EU already stating it will not make new pledges.
7
Climate finance: needs, commitments and progress
The image below explains the various climate finance estimates and goals.

developing countries (excluding China)

Source: Independent High-Level Expert Group on Climate
Finance (IHLEG)
$1.3 trillion a year by 2035
COP31 focus: first implementation update on the Baku-Belem
roadmap to $1.3T. This total is inclusive of the $300bn NCQG target.
Context: COP29 set the New Collective Quantified Goal (NCQG),
calling on all actors to scale climate finance for developing countries
to at least $1.3tn a year by 2035. No country has an assigned share,

and private capital is expected to count.
At COP31: first implementation update on the Baku-Belem roadmap
to $1.3T, based on “measurable progress” against targets including:
• a global monitoring framework to track progress in climate finance
mobilization, identify bottlenecks, and highlight emerging solutions.
• clear pathways for public, private, domestic and international
finance;
• Expanding operability, comparability, and interoperability of

climate-finance data; and
• action on grants and concessional finance, debt and fiscal space,
private-finance costs, capacity and system reform.
Are the NCQG and IHLEG aligned? IHLEG’s $1.3tn external-finance
estimate (within the $3.2tn needed excl. China) does align with the
NCQG $1.3tn goal and underpins the Baku-to-Belém Roadmap.
However, they are built differently: political target vs needs-based
estimate.
$300 billion a year by 2035
COP31 focus: what counts and who contributes. This amount

contributes towards the $1.3tn NCQG target from all actors.
Context: Developed countries agreed to lead mobilisation of at least
$300bn a year by 2035, counting public finance and the private
finance it mobilises; no country has a fixed share.
At COP31: Focus turns to how to deliver. Developing countries are
pushing for the development of action plans to chart a pathway to
$300bn and for the Article 9 work programme to be included on the
formal agenda (with emphasis on Article 9.1 – which explicitly covers

developed country finance provision).
Adaptation finance
COP31 focus: embedding and tracking the tripling pledge
Context: COP30 called for adaptation finance to at least triple by
2035, yet this was relatively vague; it did not specify a base year or a
target figure (some have suggested $120bn, i.e. 3x the 2025 goal).
At COP31: Parties need to resolve disagreement on whether to
anchor the pledge in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) and put a
finer point on the agreed Belém Adaptation Indicators that will track

it, after disagreement stalled the June Bonn talks.
Loss and Damage Fund
COP31 focus: guidance, replenishment and access
Why it matters: The Fund is the only dedicated channel for
irreversible climate impacts, yet it has no annual funding target and
resources remain far below demand.
At COP31: Focus will be on its long-term funding strategy, with the
first replenishment cycle set to begin in 2027. $342m made available
in the first funding window, which offers $5–20m grants with at least

half reserved for SIDS and LDCs; but the voluntary nature of
payments, disputes over who pays and sensitivity around
“compensation” make major new pledges in Antalya unlikely.

Hurdles to these
targets
Grants vs loans

Developing countries want grants (especially fo r
adaptation), citing debt burdens and historical
responsibility; funders favour loans and blended
structures that include private capital.
Pressure on development banks
The US is pushing MDBs (e.g. the World Bank), to drop
their climate focus, according to the World Resources
Institute (WRI). Developing countries want MDB reform
to deliver more investment and faster disbursement.
Competing budget pressures

Donors are diverting aid to fund domestic priorities
such as defence. There is also the reality that climate is
now a lower priority for some governments.
Reliance on private capital
The IHLEG estimates ~½ the $1.3tn could come from
private sources. Private capital is looking for regulation,
insurance and more guarantees to reduce risk.

### Page 8

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (1  o f 2 )
Adaptation will be one of the most important, and more divisive, negotiating topics
Adaptation is also set to be on the formal negotiating agenda at COP31. UN warnings that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable (at least temporarily), have
dampened morale for climate activists. As it relates to the COP agenda on adaptation, parties appear deadlocked on whether the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 should be

included in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) text. This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal to investors the scale and timing of future public and blended finance flows.
The role of adaptation in the COP process
The Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) was established under the Paris Agreement to enhance
adaptive capacity, strengthen resilience and reduce vulnerability to climate change. At COP30
last year, countries adopted 59 indicators (still being refined) to measure progress against the

goal. They also agreed a goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035. The pledge was vague: it set
neither a concrete figure nor a baseline year (a potential annual target of $120bn would be
triple the previous goal of c.$40bn by 2025).
Why GGA talks were deadlocked at the June climate meetings
Finance was the main sticking point at the June Bonn meetings. Developing countries wanted
the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 referenced in the GGA text; countries

including Canada, Norway and Japan opposed. Parties also split on whether the indicators task
force should be expert- or party-led, so no GGA text was agreed and the decision passes to
COP31. Moving the Adaptation Fund to serve the Paris Agreement exclusively (funded by a 5%
share of proceeds from Article 6.4 credits) stalled in Bonn over the composition of its board;
talks will resume at COP31.
What is needed at COP31 to unlock adaptation progress

Adaptation is on the provisional COP31 agenda, including the tripling adaptation finance goal
(though this may get blocked). Some parties want it explicitly referenced in the final COP31
GGA decision; this should become clearer when the agenda is adopted as COP31 opens on 9
Nov. At issue: the tripling pledge still lacks a baseline and named contributors. At COP30,
developed nations resisted new finance obligations, while the EU and many others pushed for

a roadmap to transition away from fossil fuels, which a group of mostly oil- and gas-producing
countries blocked. The result was a looser tripling goal and no agreed fossil-fuel roadmap.
Developing countries are unlikely to give ground, as adaptation relies far more on public
funding than mitigation, so a repeat of COP30’s finance vs. mitigation debate is plausible.
Sources: United Nations, UNEP, Carbon Brief, European Parliament (COP30 outcomes briefing, December 2025)

### Page 9

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (2  o f 2 )
The EU’s proposed Climate Resilience Law plans for a 3°C world, while reiterating commitment to net zero 2050
The EU Commission’s recently proposed Climate Resilience Law would set a reference global warming trajectory of 3°C, providing a benchmark for member states’ adaptation planning. The EU
states it remains committed to reaching net zero by 2050, but some at COP will likely view the proposal as accepting overshoot. With the EU also pointing to an estimated need for EUR70bn in

annual adaptation investment, this provides an interesting backdrop as COP31 seeks to resume adaptation negotiations which stalled earlier this year, primarily due to finance.
What the Commission proposed
On 27 October the Commission presented its EU Framework for Climate Resilience, anchored
by a proposed European Climate Resilience Law. With current global policy commitments
pointing to around 2°C of warming by 2050 and 3°C by 2100, the law would set a common

reference trajectory of 1.5°C in 2030, 2°C in 2050 and 3°C in 2100, with this regarded as the
“baseline assumption”. Member states would be required to use it in national climate risk
assessments and adopt a single national resilience plan by end-2030.
Why it matters: the EU is planning for overshoot
The EU highlighted warnings that overshoot of the 1.5°C goal is now “unavoidable” (even if just
temporarily) as necessitating policy action, even as the EU says it is still committed to climate

neutrality by 2050. The Commission links the plan to Europe’s hottest summer on record and
estimates annual economic losses from climate extremes of €50bn over the past four years,
adding that output losses caused by climate change go far beyond this. It says EU adaptation
needs are ~€70bn a year through to 2050, with Europe warming at 2x the global rate.
What it means for COP31
The proposal demonstrates that warnings on the implications of a significant overshoot of the

Paris Agreement global warming limits are moving from scientific theory to real government
policy. While the opening line of the new EU framework emphasized it remains committed to
reaching net zero by 2050, some may view a 3°C planning baseline as accepting overshoot.
The headline €70bn annual EU adaptation finance requirement also provides an interesting
backdrop heading into Global Goal on Adaptation talks given they are stalled largely due to

disagreement over explicit links to finance in the GGA text. For context, the UNEP estimates
annual adaptation needs for developing countries of $310–365bn by 2035. Ultimately, we
expect adaptation focus to continue growing as warming impacts are increasingly felt and
societal calls for relief grow louder. Corporates will need to consider potential strategic
impacts, while some investors have already begun raising their baseline warming forecasts.

### Page 10

TO P I C  4 | EL EC TR I F I C ATI O N
Türkiye is championing a voluntary pledge to lift electrification from 23% to 35% by 2035
Electrification is expected to be at the centre of this “implementation COP”, targeting a rise in electricity’s share of global final energy use from 23% to 35% by 2035. We expect support for this
action given it is being positioned alongside energy security, but we also expect discussion and debate over how it is delivered (e.g., the role of renewables, nuclear and natural gas), alongside

concerns over clean tech dependency in supply chains. China and the EU have expressed support but not yet committed.
Some may brand this the electrification COP
The Action Agenda pledge aims to raise electricity's share of global final energy from 23%
today to 35% by 2035. The pledge was launched in Bonn this past June and formalised as the
"35-by-35 Global Electrification Pledge" in New York on 21 September.
China and the EU have signaled support, but have not yet signed the pledge

Electrification fits modern day energy-security goals making it a viable target for this year’s
COP. China’s cleantech dominance means it has a further incentive to support initiatives that
seek to increase the pace of global electrification. It also aligns closely with China's existing
development trajectory. While the 35% goal is based on Paris-aligned pathways supported by
IEA and IRENA analysis, the numerical target itself does not require the additional electricity to

come from non fossil fuel sources. This has invited some criticism, though it may make it
easier for some nations to sign the non-binding pledge.
Alignment with the voluntary renewables pledge
Recall that COP28 called for tripling global renewable power capacity by 2030 in COP28’s
formal Global Stocktake decision, equivalent to around 11.2 TW. IRENA’s September 2026
assessment puts capacity at 5.15 TW at end-2025. Record additions of 693 GW in 2025 remain

below the 1,200 GW needed annually in 2026–30. The 35-by-35 pledge complements this
supply goal by shifting transport, buildings and industry towards electricity, enabling
renewable power to replace fossil fuels. Its emissions benefit still depends on cleaner
generation.
We view the goal positively, but delivery needs grid enhancements.
IRENA counts ~2,500 GW of renewable, storage and large-load projects awaiting connection
and says annual investment to strengthen grid infrastructure and enhance system flexibility

must rise from ~$500bn in 2025 to around $1tn through 2035, and $1.2tn thereafter. That
said, signatories signal support, but domestic delivery plans will determine investment.
Sources: UNFCCC, COP31 Presidency, IEA, IRENA

TO P I C  5   |   F O S S I L  F U EL S
The fossil fuel transition roadmap track is growing outside the formal COP process
COP30 failed to agree a fossil fuel transition roadmap, despite support from more than 80 countries. Brazil’s COP30 presidency is therefore developing a roadmap outside formal UN
negotiations to be presented ahead of COP31. Separately, Colombia and the Netherlands convened a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 countries in Santa Marta in April 2026 to explore practical

steps towards a fossil fuel phase-out. This parallel forum allows willing countries to cooperate without needing consensus from all COP parties.
Sources: Carbon Brief, Santa Marta conference report
